# Deferred obligations ledger

> **Maintained companion — NOT a generated ticket.** This file is hand-maintained and committed. It
> is not produced by `decompose-spec` and must be **preserved across any ticket regeneration** — never
> overwritten or deleted. Rows are append-only (see rules below). It has no `NN_` sequence prefix
> precisely so it is not mistaken for a chain ticket.

Durable, in-repo record of **acceptance criteria and deliverables that a ticket could not fully
satisfy at implementation time** — almost always because they need a live GCP deploy or a HUMAN
prerequisite (Phase 0a / H0 etc.) that was not yet done. Each row names how it was proxied now, what
unblocks it, and exactly how to verify it when unblocked.

> **Machine ledger (build-memory v2, MAINT.1 / ADR-004):** the committed build state that reads these rows is
> `docs/build/LEDGER.md` (CURRENT STATE / OPEN FINDINGS / RETURN PASS / PHASE LOG); `docs/build/BUILD_INDEX.md`
> is one row per PR. `.agents/scratch/` is retired.

**This file is the source of truth for "what still owes verification."** It is read at the start of
every `implement-spec` run (via `AGENTS.md`). The rules:

1. **Every run, first:** read this file. If the ticket you are about to implement — or a prerequisite
   it depends on (e.g. H0 finishing, or an earlier ticket landing the egress-proxy SG) — unblocks any
   `OPEN` row, **closing that row is part of your run**: verify it for real, then flip it to `DONE`
   with the date and evidence (commit/PR).
2. **Never delete a row.** Flip `OPEN` → `DONE` (verified) or `WONTFIX` (with a reason). History stays.
3. **When you defer something new,** append a row here in the same run that defers it. A deferral that
   is not in this file did not happen.
4. **Backstop gates:** the phase-exit gates in `docs/tickets` (e.g. `16a_GATE-G1__phase0-exit.md`) must
   refuse to pass while any `OPEN` row scoped to that phase remains. Treat an open row as gate-blocking.

Status values: `OPEN` (owed) · `PARTIAL` (some of it landed; the rest still owed) · `DONE` (verified — add
date + evidence) · `WONTFIX` (add reason) · `ACCEPTED-SKELETON` (intentionally minimal for now; revisit at the
named ticket). A row's status is the first of these keywords that appears in its status cell (the rest of the
cell is free-form evidence prose).

---

| D-P0.10-2 | **`queued`-stranded sweep after a crash between commit and enqueue** — `resolveGate`/`handleGateExpiry`/`skipGateByPolicy` transition the run to `queued` inside the tx and `queue.send(run.resume\|run.start)` AFTER commit (the pg-boss send is not transactional). A crash in that window leaves the run `queued` with no job; the reaper is heartbeat-driven (a `queued` run has no heartbeat), so nothing recovers it. Worst case: a `confirm_cost` consent commits its hold then the send is lost → a stranded run holding credits | Same class as **D-P0.08-4** (stuck-`provisioning` sweep). Both are the enqueue-after-commit crash window; the correct fix is a transactional outbox (a job row written in the tx, relayed post-commit) or a system-scoped sweep of non-terminal runs with no active pg-boss job — live-infra work · → **PRB.14** code half / **PRB.17** live leg (2026-09-16 plan) | **D-P0.08-4** / the live worker (a system-scoped sweep or a pg-boss transactional outbox) | A `run.reap`-adjacent sweep re-enqueues `queued`/`awaiting_consent→queued` runs with `state_since` older than a threshold and no active job (and, for a stranded consent, the hold is either used by the launched run or released); assert a killed process mid-resolve recovers | Enqueue is singleton-keyed (a client retry that finds the gate resolved returns `already_resolved` without re-sending); the reaper + pg-boss `run.start` retry cover the common cases; only the commit→send crash window is uncovered — identical to the P0.08 pattern | **CODE-half DONE (2026-09-17, PRB.14).** `sweepStrandedRuns` legs B+C: `queued` runs older than 60s get their launch job re-sent (singleton-keyed on the run id — a still-active job dedupes, so a re-sweep is a no-op; attempt>1 sends `run.resume` so `reDebitOnResume` still fires; a stranded consent's hold is CONSUMED by the relaunched run, never released), and unresolved gates on parked (`waiting_gate`/`awaiting_consent`) runs get their expiry job re-armed (an overdue `confirm_cost` then cancels + releases the hold — proven end-to-end through `handleGateExpiry`). Registered on the same 1-min `run.reap` cron. **DONE (2026-09-18, PRB.17 live leg).** Planted `queued`-stranded fixture `01PRB17STRANDEDQUEUED00` (no job row) → worker log 16:40:11 `stranded-sweep: {"kind":"queued_reenqueued","runId":"01PRB17STRANDEDQUEUED00","attempt":1}` → `run.start` job created 16:40:11 / consumed 16:40:12 — the enqueue-loss window recovers on prod. The `gate_rearmed` half also observed live (16:30–16:34, gate `01M2TNKY14HSP3ESJH8Y1NK8HW` re-armed repeatedly while unresolved). **2026-09-25 (RCN.1):** the re-armed expiry jobs then fired — all five `gate.expiry` rows for that gate `started`/`completed` 16:29:29–16:29:37Z at the 16:29:27Z deadline (prod `pgboss.job`); scheduler backlog clear (0 stale `created` jobs; `run.reap` completing every ~30 s after PRB.25). |

> spend). The environment for this run had **no GCP ADC, no Pulumi CLI, no vendored SDK, no stated
> budget, and no provider no-training verification** — the operator prerequisites the ticket names as
> hard blockers — so Stage B could not proceed. **GATE G1 stays blocked while any row below (and the

## P0.15c — Live client bindings + engine E2E (PR #19)

> **`16_P0.15c` executes P0.15b's Stage B. Stage A (offline, PR-able — DONE) wrote the thin live
> shell behind EVERY DI seam** — the "constructed at deploy" call-sites P0.15b's recorded deviation deferred.
>
> **STAGE B WAS EXECUTED LIVE (2026-09-03, operator-authorized: full dev deploy, $300 ceiling, $10/run cap).**
> The full `dev` stack was deployed on `rhema-507501`, all three images built + pushed, migrations + seed run
> live, and the run pipeline driven end-to-end — **but it did NOT reach a green Review**, and G1 is NOT ready.
> The deploy + orchestration path is proven live up to the agent-execution boundary; it is **blocked there by
> a genuine Stage-A implementation gap: nobody builds the harness `RUN_SPEC` (the phase request + hydration
> manifest + hook policy the agent executes)** — see the new row D-P0.15c-6. Executing Stage B surfaced ~16
> deploy-only bugs (invisible to the offline typecheck/preview) — landed in commit `10d1df9`. This overturns
> the ticket premise that Stage B is "execution, not construction": completing it needs real orchestration
> code (RUN_SPEC assembly) + infra re-platforming (proxy MIG, sandbox-node egress), best done as reviewed PRs.
> **These outstanding rows (D-P0.15c-6/7/8 + the live re-run legs) are synthesized into one implementable
> ticket — `docs/tickets/16_P0.15d__live-runspec-infra-and-green-review.md` (Stage A offline construction = one
> implement-spec session; Stage B operator-gated live execution).**
> The billable stack was torn down after the run to honor the cost-conscious mandate; re-deploy is a proven
> ~30-min path (see the exit readout runbook). GATE G1 stays blocked while any G1-scoped OPEN row remains.
>
> **Live-proven this session (evidence):** `pulumi up` stood up AlloyDB (PG 17.9) + GKE + Redis + GCS +
> Secret Manager + Cloud Run + the network; the sandbox subnet has no NAT / no default route (D-P0.01-2
> extended to the full stack). `applyMigrations` (0001–0015) + `seedRealProjects` ran on live AlloyDB → 3
> real projects (proj_cr/pv/sd) + 6 drafts + protocols (**closes D-P0.02-1 live + D-P0.03-5**). The api +
> egress-proxy ran as containers in-VPC; the worker (RS256 mint + GKE launcher) provisioned a run and
> **launched the two-uid run-task Job onto GKE**; the pod scheduled, pulled the digest-pinned image, and the
> harness executed — reaching `/work` materialization before failing for lack of a `RUN_SPEC`. No paid
> Anthropic inference occurred (the run never reached the SDK), so the cap-402 / SDK cross-check / cited⇒fetched
> / sidecar / attestation criteria are **still unverified on the wire**.
>

> re-run) and surfaced two follow-ups below. Stage B (the live re-deploy + paid Reviews) stays [OPERATOR GATE]d

> `termsRef` captured in the ticket) — recorded live as the pilot's first step, prerequisite (b) still needed.

| D-P0.15d-1 | **Reduced-rigor phase-set derivation in `buildHarnessRunSpec`.** With no explicit `phase` pin, the assembler derives the phase to run as `index === committed-phase count` over the FULL workflow manifest. For a REDUCED-rigor run (phases opted out via a manifest `optional` notch) this can select an opted-out phase and report the full `of`. | Deriving a run's ACTIVE phase set from its rigor (the §6.5/P0.06 rigor→`optOutFlag` mapping) is not available in the orchestrator yet, and the `optional: Rigor` semantics are not pinned; guessing risks the happy path | The §6.5 rigor→active-phase filter (P0.06/§6.5), or run-creation freezing the active phase list into `input_snapshot` | Filter the manifest to the run's active phases by rigor, derive the current phase + `of` from that list; assert a Quick/Standard run with an opted-out phase runs the next ENABLED phase | The default full-rigor run (the first-green milestone) is correct; an explicit `phase:"N"` pin is always honored (both tested, `runspec.test.ts`); only reduced-rigor auto-derivation is affected | **DONE (P0.15h Stage B, commit `0514679`).** `buildHarnessRunSpec` now selects the next phase by POSITION in the run's ACTIVE phase set — the manifest phases minus those whose `optOutFlag` is set `false` in the frozen inputs. Opted-out phases produce no committed step, so the committed-phase COUNT is exactly the next active phase's position; `of` stays the full manifest length so the delivery trigger (`phase.index === of-1`, keyed to the always-active final `letter`) is unaffected, and a `phase` pin still wins. Unit-tested (`runspec.test.ts`: a `second_read=false` run at committed=4 selects `argument`, skipping `second_read`). This is what lets G1 deliver with `second_read` opted out ([[D-P0.15h-5]]). |

| D-P0.15d-4 | **STAGE-A CONSTRUCTION DONE (2026-09-04, P0.15e).** The pod-model COMMIT/DELIVER loop is unbuilt (the true green-Review blocker, surfaced live 2026-09-04). After the pod's `agent-entry` runs `engine.startPhase` and streams events, NOTHING reads the committed `.rhema/phase-result.json`, evaluates the completion oracle, calls `commitStep`, and (on the final phase) posts the delivering dossier to `POST /internal/runs/:id/deliver`. `harness-main.ts` forwards events + exits; the pod never advances/commits the run. So even a fully deployed stack + a running agent cannot produce a GREEN Review (a Review requires the phases to commit + the dossier to deliver + attest). This is the NEXT RUN_SPEC-class construction gap — the server-side/pod completion-oracle→commit→deliver loop (§12.4 steps 6–8) in the two-uid pod model. | The P0.15c architectural divergence: the pod drives `startPhase` in-process, but the orchestrator's `runPhase`/`commitStep`/`deliverRun` (all built + unit-tested) are never invoked after the pod's phase runs — no code bridges pod-phase-end → server commit. It is core orchestration (like D-P0.15c-6 was), not ops. | **A PR** building the pod-side (or harness-side) commit driver: read the phase-result + artifacts, run the completion oracle (`evaluateOracle`), call the token-authed `commit`/`fail`/`deliver` internal routes per phase, loop to the next phase (re-fetch the run spec). Then a live re-run reaches a green Review. | After it lands: a live Review commits each phase + delivers, `assessPhase0ExitForRun` reads `violations=0`/`citedFetched.ok`/`sidecars.allOk`. | Every server-side piece exists + is unit-tested (`runPhase`, `evaluateOracle`, `commitStep`, `deliverRun`, `buildHarnessRunSpec`); only the pod→server commit BRIDGE is missing. The infra + run-spec + images are all validated live (P0.15d) — this is the last construction blocker before a green Review. | **DONE (2026-09-25, RCN.1) — live-proven (see appended note). Prior record: STAGE-A DONE (2026-09-04, P0.15e); OPEN for the live re-run.** The harness now drives `runCommitLoop` (`apps/run-task/harness-loop.ts`): per phase it fetches the run spec, runs the phase (`agent-entry`, which writes `.rhema/agent-result.json`), reads `/work` (`work-reader.ts` `buildPhaseOutputs`), evaluates the orchestrator's PURE oracle (`evaluateOracle`, one guided retry), then calls the token-authed routes — a new `POST .../steps/:key/harvest` (persists produced versions + provisional `sdk_stream`/`usage.tick`, D-P0.15d-3), `commit` with a `CommitNext` from the new pure `decideCommitNext` (advance/gate/finalize via `gateAfter`+`resolveGatePolicy`), and on the final phase assembles `DeliveryArgs` from `/work`+the run spec's server-side delivery facts and posts `deliver` (bare `finalize` for a pinned `phase:N`/no-draft run). Unit-tested over FakeEngine seams (advance/gate+resume/`phase:N`/guided-retry/no-secret-leak) AND end-to-end through the REAL api routes + PGlite (`integration/pod-loop.test.ts`: intake→gate; letter→deliver→`completed` with `assessPhase0ExitForRun` green). ## P0.15f — pod-log observability + agent→proxy path + worker GKE (Stage A landed; Stage B operator-gated)

> **P0.15f Stage A (offline, PR-able — DONE this run) built the three diagnosis/fix enablers P0.15e's live run
> needed, annotated in place above (D-P0.15e-2/3/4 + D-P0.07-5):** (1) a readable agent-error observability sink
> (agent-entry captures a thrown SDK/engine error → work-reader threads it → the commit loop folds it into the
> `failStep` reason → `failStep` emits an INTERNAL `engine.error` run event in the step-fail tx), so an agent
> hang/crash is queryable from `run_events`/`steps` WITHOUT pod exec — the P0.15e blind spot; (2) the worker
> GKE-Job launcher without an out-of-band step (option b: a metadata-token `KubeConfig` refreshed per launch,
> keeping the proven Cloud Run worker topology); (3) the proxy SSE/streaming pass (`streamProxyRequest` streams
> the response through incrementally + meters the accumulated body once — the buffered `handleProxyRequest` stays
> byte-identical). Konnectivity egress (item 4) was determined NOT an offline IaC gap (network.ts already allows
> sandbox→master 443+10250); the sink is the primary mechanism, konnectivity restoration is a Stage-B ops matter.
>
> **Stage B WAS EXECUTED LIVE (2026-09-04, operator-authorized $50 ceiling / $10-per-run; pilot SKIPPED).** The
> full `dev` stack was re-deployed and a Review run driven to the agent-execution boundary WITH POD LOGS in hand
> (the P0.15e blind spot is gone). **$0 inference** (no run reached the model). Live findings:
>
> - **D-P0.15e-3 — CLOSED LIVE:** the Cloud Run worker launched the two-uid GKE Job via the metadata-token
>   `KubeConfig` launcher with NO out-of-band step. Job + pod ran.
> - **D-P0.15e-4 (pod logs) — REAL IaC GAPS FOUND + FIXED (my Stage-A "not an IaC gap" call was WRONG):**
>   konnectivity-agent dials `<master>:8132` (not just 443/10250) → firewall missing 8132 → "No agent available".
>   Then `kubectl logs` failed "dial `<node>`:10250 i/o timeout" → node→node kubelet egress blocked by deny-all.
>   Added BOTH to `network.ts` (`sandbox-allow-gke-master` +8132; new `sandbox-node-kubelet` egress) + applied
>   live via gcloud → `kubectl logs`/`exec` WORK. **Pod-log observability is RESTORED on `dev`.**
> - **D-P0.15e-2 (agent→proxy) — TRUE ROOT CAUSE, and it is a CHAIN, NOT a single hang (overturns the P0.15e
>   "SDK hang / proxy-buffering" hypothesis):** with logs, the pod fails `spawn … ENOENT` — **the agent was
>   NEVER SPAWNED.** (1) `buildAgentLaunch` spawned bare `setpriv`/`node`, but the scrubbed agent env forbids
>   `PATH` → unresolvable; FIXED (absolute `/usr/bin/setpriv` + `process.execPath`). (2) the agent cwd
>   `/work/project` was never created (`materializeWork` writes `/work` root) → `spawn` ENOENT on the missing
>   cwd; FIXED (cwd = workDir + mkdir). After both, the agent process SPAWNS live — then the NEXT link surfaced:
>   **`setpriv: setresgid failed: Operation not permitted`** (the non-root harness lacks ambient CAP_SETUID/SETGID
>   to drop to the agent uid — the two-uid capability model, D-P0.15f-1), and behind THAT the **per-run proxy
>   credential is never injected** into the agent's request (D-P0.15f-2). A green phase needs those + the untested
>   first live SDK exercise (D-P0.06-1). Also found + fixed: the pod NetworkPolicy blocked the WI metadata server
>   (169.254.169.254) the harness needs for GCS auth (added to `infra/k8s/20-run-task-egress-policy.yaml`).
>
> **The setpriv/cwd/konnectivity/NetworkPolicy fixes are committed on this branch (code + IaC).** The billable
> stack was torn down after the run (cost mandate). **GATE G1 stays blocked** — D-P0.15e-2 stays OPEN (the chain
> is not fully closed), the live-pilot rows stay OPEN (pilot SKIPPED), and D-P0.15f-1/2 are new construction
> blockers. See `docs/phase0-exit-readout.md` § "Stage B — live execution (2026-09-04, P0.15f)".

Prior record: **ROOT-CAUSED + OFFLINE FIX LANDED (P0.15h):** the SDK's PreToolUse hook runs in the AGENT process (uid 10002) but the daemon socket was `chmod 0600` (harness-only, uid 10001) → the agent got EACCES on connect, the hook threw, and the SDK fell through to ALLOW (0 decisions live; an out-of-namespace write succeeded). Envelope now translated at `buildPreToolUseHook`, daemon fails closed on malformed input, socket 0666, buffered audit ships via `POST /internal/runs/:id/audit`. Prod run `01M2TRYC9…` — **66 `access_log` rows (25 allow / 41 deny)** incl. | D-P0.15h-3 | **Worker GKE-launcher cold-start is a ~5–7 min event-loop-BLOCKING import.** The first `run.start` on a fresh Cloud Run worker instance does `await import("@kubernetes/client-node")` (+ KubeConfig), which on a cold instance blocks the event loop for minutes (starving pg-boss if done eagerly). P0.15h added timeout+retry and reverted an eager-warm (lazy-in-handler works: pg-boss delivers → `provisioning` → slow import → launch), but every worker (re)deploy pays the slow first launch. | The k8s client import is heavy + apparently event-loop-blocking on cold Cloud Run; setTimeout-based timeouts can't fire during the block. | A PR: pre-warm the client at BUILD time / top-level before pg-boss starts, use a smaller/lazier k8s API surface, run the import in a worker thread, or bump the worker CPU/startup-boost — so the first launch is fast. | On `dev`: a fresh worker launches its first Job within the 45 s SLO. | Launches are RELIABLE now (just slow on the first); timeout+retry bounds a true hang. | **DONE — ROOT-CAUSED + FIXED (P0.15h Stage B, 2026-09-09).** The "event-loop-blocking import" was actually Cloud Run CPU THROTTLING: the worker ran at the default (CPU allocated only during a request) + 512Mi, so the background pg-boss handler's `@kubernetes/client-node` import crawled and the first launch hung ~9 min. `cpuIdle:false` was already in IaC but a gcloud image-swap had reverted it live. Fix: `--no-cpu-throttling --memory=1Gi` live, and IaC now pins `resources: {cpuIdle:false, startupCpuBoost:true, limits:{cpu:"1", memory:"1Gi"}}` (commit `4f280c3`) — the cold import then finishes in SECONDS and the first launch is fast. |

Prior record: **PARTIAL — FIXED (P0.15h Stage B, commit `511f024`) — `agent-entry` sets `process.umask(0o000)` so agent-created files are 0666/0777 and the harness can overwrite them across same-pod phases. | D-P0.15h-8 | **The fact-check agent does NO real, logged web fetches → cited_not_fetched blocks delivery (the last green-Review blocker).** Live P0.15h Stage B: `usage_records.searches = 0`, `fetches = 0`; `fetch_log` holds only `inference` rows; so the claim ledger's citations are model-KNOWLEDGE, not fetch-backed, and `runDeliveryTransaction` correctly throws `DeliveryValidationError("cited_not_fetched")` (§12.8(c)). Root cause: `packages/engine/src/options.ts` lists `WebSearch`/`WebFetch` only as allowed-tool NAMES; nothing adds Anthropic's SERVER-SIDE web-search tool (`web_search_20250305`) to the request `tools[]`, so the model has no working search tool (client-side WebFetch can't reach arbitrary URLs under the sandbox network lock). The egress-proxy is already built to expect the tool (`url-policy.ts` `injectUrlPolicyIntoBody`/`isServerWebTool`) and — as of D-P0.15h-7 — to log its result URLs. | Enabling the server-side web-search tool in the vendored SDK request is a real design piece (SDK-vs-raw-API config, web_search vs web_fetch, domain-policy + metering interaction), best researched + built as a focused PR. Server-side web_search needs NO pod egress (Anthropic fetches inside the api.anthropic.com call the proxy already allows). | **A dedicated ticket — `docs/tickets/16_P0.15i__agent-web-research-in-sandbox.md`**: enable the server-side web-search tool in the SDK options, confirm the proxy scopes + logs it, and assert `cited⇒fetched` passes with real provenance while still rejecting a genuinely unfetched citation. | On `dev`: a Review's verification does real web research (`searches > 0`), the proxy logs the source URLs, and a COMPLETE green Review DELIVERS with a `correspondence_items` row + `assessPhase0ExitForRun` green. | The whole pipeline (agent launch → all 6 phases → harvest → deliver path) is proven; only the fetch-provenance capability is missing; the proxy-side logging (D-P0.15h-7) is already in. | **DONE (2026-09-25, RCN.1) — live-proven (see appended note). Prior record: STAGE-A CONSTRUCTION DONE (P0.15i, 2026-09-09); OPEN for the live Stage-B verification (operator gate).** The proxy is now the GUARANTOR of server-side web research (approach #3): `ensureServerWebTools` (`apps/egress-proxy/src/url-policy.ts`) ADDS Anthropic's server `web_search` (basic `web_search_20250305` — GA, no beta header, the broadest-compatible variant across the whole Rhema model line incl. `claude-haiku-4-5`) to every `POST /v1/messages` body that lacks one, then `injectUrlPolicyIntoBody` scopes its domains; wired into the shared `prepareProxyRequest` (buffered + SSE). `web_fetch` is off by default (its basic `web_fetch_20250910` variant needs the `web-fetch-2025-09-10` beta header the proxy does not yet set — enabling it is a documented follow-up; web_search alone gives `cited⇒fetched` its provenance). The client-side `WebFetch` (unroutable under the sandbox's proxy-only egress) is dropped from `ALLOWED_TOOLS` + the `researcher` roster (AC1); metering (`web_search_requests`→`usage_records.searches`) + result-URL logging ([[D-P0.15h-7]]) were already in. Offline ACs green: request `tools[]` carries the server tool (unit), proxy scopes+logs it (unit, buffered+SSE), `validateCitedFetched` passes on proxy-logged provenance and still fails an unfetched citation (AC2, guard intact). **AC3 PROVEN LIVE (P0.15i Stage B, 2026-09-09, PR #25).** A live Review on `dev` (base tier, real inference) did REAL server-side web research: `usage_records.searches = 46`, `fetch_log` = **197 `kind='fetch'` result-URL rows + 23 `kind='search'` rows** — substantive sources (SEC filings, NCBI/RePEc papers, Wikipedia, essays) with topic-matched fact-check queries — exactly the proxy fetch trail `validateCitedFetched` (§12.8(c)) resolves citations against, so `cited_not_fetched` no longer fails a real fact-check. Three live-diagnosed fixes made it work (commit `262133a`): (1) drop client `WebSearch` too — the CLI declares it in `tools[]` regardless of the allowlist and the model calls it in preference to the server tool; (2) the proxy STRIPS the CLI's client `WebSearch`/`WebFetch` from `tools[]` so only the injected server `web_search` remains (searches 0→12+); (3) the proxy strips `accept-encoding` (`NEVER_FORWARD`) — with gzip on, the SDK decompressed the response but the proxy metered the COMPRESSED bytes, so its parsers logged zero usage/fetch rows even though the search ran. **AC4 (a COMPLETE 6-phase Review DELIVERS with a `correspondence_items` row + `assessPhase0ExitForRun` green) NOT yet observed: the proving run reached the letter-adjacent phases but WEDGED in a later subagent phase (discourse/argument) — no active pod, run stuck `running` — a phase-loop/scoped-subagent stall orthogonal to P0.15i (adjacent to [[D-P0.15h-5]]). Scoped to a follow-up (D-P0.15i-1). G1 still needs the full green delivery + [[D-P0.15-1]].** **2026-09-25 (RCN.1):** discharged — the Stage-B verification ran on prod: delivered fact_check `01M2TZ8E…` + three delivered reviews carry real fetch/search trails (`fetch_log` fetch×2,337 / search×240), and deliveries landed `correspondence_items` rows with cited⇒fetched resolved via the Path-B strip (D-P0.15i-2). |

Unit-tested (stamp/forward/stream/502 + the 403-without-credential reference rule). Live `usage_records{source:'proxy'}` confirmation is Stage B — rides D-P1.22-2's bundle. **2026-09-25 (RCN.1):** live-proven on prod — `usage_records` (source='proxy') ×2,434 rows (104 for delivered run `01M36663…`); agent calls are metered at the proxy, which requires the stamped `x-rhema-proxy-credential` (a request without it 403s `egress.denied`). Residual — an expiry firing on a *live* gate driving an actual cancel transition — stays owed → **STB.3**'s review-quick leg. **DONE (2026-09-26, STB.3 — prod):** `confirm_cost` gate `01M3DQ59YHTWKEJCYS2Z3R0CAD` (run `01M3DQ59XW90DYHWTRAMP3XT7P`, `on_expiry:cancel`, deadline 02:05:38.752Z) — the stranded-sweep re-armed it live (`gate_rearmed` log lines 02:02:27→02:05:21) → `gate.expiry` consumed: `{"action":"resolved","onExpiry":"cancel","state":"cancelled"}` 02:05:41.205Z → `run.state_changed(cancelled)` 02:05:41.185Z; two follow-up expiry ticks no-op'd `already_resolved`; the `run_cancelled_recovered` inbox row landed (`visible_at` = resolution). Scope note: the memo run's `approve_scope` gate is `on_expiry:wait` with `deadline_at:null` — informational, never expires; the leg used `confirm_cost` as the deadline-bearing cancel gate the row describes. |
| D-PRB.16-5 | **Committed-debit vs proxy-truth divergence at terminal settle (spend-accounting question, live-observed).** QV-1 `01M2T65W…` settled `spent_credits=794` (proxy truth) but the hold released `788−175=613` against committed debits only — 619 cr of proxy-metered burn on never-committed attempts is recorded in `usage_records` but not charged via `credit_ledger`. | Whether failed-attempt proxy burn should debit the workspace is a product/accounting decision, not an implementation bug to fix unilaterally. | Operator ruling on the intended accounting (cap-enforcement meter vs invoiced debits); then code if the ruling says debit. | Reconcile a capped run: `credit_ledger` debits vs `usage_records` proxy Σ vs `runs.spent_credits` vs the hold `release` row — all four agree with the ruling. | Documented in the PRB.16b run ledger's Spend section with the live numbers. **2026-09-19 (PRB.19 Stage B) — second data point, same shape:** Revise `01M2VDHN2ZFDV1SVDX2XN257NN` — hold 1600 → debits 176+145+187+133+170 = **811** → release **789**; `usage_records` proxy Σ **1668** = reconciled `runs.spent_credits` (1668), `sdk_stream` 811, `sdk_estimate` 927; **857 cr proxy-metered but uncharged** (failed-attempt + debug-phase inference). Cap raised 1600→3500 mid-debug; the hold stayed 1600. | **DONE — operator ruling 2026-09-22.** (a) **Cap-meter only:** uncommitted burn (retries/failed attempts) does NOT debit the workspace — it is cap-enforcement metering absorbed as platform cost, bounded by the hold. Users are invoiced on committed debits only, preserving "never a silent bill". No code change. (b) **Rate-card gap expected:** the ~18% divergence between `usage_records` proxy Σ (list-priced SDK cost) and committed debits (platform rate table) is by-design platform pricing, not a defect. All four reconciliation numbers (`credit_ledger` debits, `usage_records` proxy Σ, `runs.spent_credits`, hold `release`) agree with this ruling. **Superseded 2026-10-02 (ECON.1; ADR-014 under `debitMode=metered`; ADR-031):** the "cap-meter only" ruling governed the committed-debit world it was written for; under `metered`, REQ-SPEC-32(a) makes the proxy meter the invoice, so completed and user-cancelled runs settle to `min(metered, cap)` exactly (over-debit refunds `metered_over_debit`; the residue sweep re-settles late arrivals ≤72 h). What survives on every mode: failed-run burn is platform fault — refund + absorb, never charged; unrecorded in-flight burn stays COGS (REQ-SPEC-24(8)); the 2× breaker is runtime-only. |
| D-PRB.17-1 | **Stripe-originated purchase leg (live-or-test per H6) + real Payment Link.** The deployed webhook's verify→insert→enqueue→process→`grant(topup)` chain IS proven on prod (constructed event signed by the configured `whsec` → `stripe_events` + 2,500cr grant + replay no-op, `pi_prb17_test_1`; exhaustion-panel `open_payment_link`/`topup` telemetry + 302 to the configured link), but the Stripe-ORIGINATED half — mint a test-mode webhook endpoint via the Stripe API, drive a real `checkout.session.completed`, mint a real `buy.stripe.com` Payment Link — is impossible: **no Stripe API credential exists in the project.** | HUMAN-H6 (2026-09-18) answered "test mode" for the mode question, but `rhema-prod-stripe-secret` v1 is a `PLACEHOLDER_…` value, `rhema-dev-stripe-secret` has zero versions, and `rhema-prod-stripe-webhook-secret` v1 is `whsec_PLACEH…` — the 2026-09-17 "copy 5 dev→prod" seed copied placeholders. No `sk_test_`/`sk_live_` is reachable (env, keychain, dotenv, all 10 gcloud projects checked). Per the autonomy contract this is surfaced, never fabricated. | **Operator** (a real Stripe test-mode secret key + webhook signing secret seeded to Secret Manager; live mode iff the H6 activation check passes) — then re-run the leg. | With a real `sk_test_…`: `POST /v1/webhook_endpoints` a test endpoint → copy the returned `whsec_…` into `rhema-prod-stripe-webhook-secret` → mint a Payment Link → drive a real purchase → assert `stripe_events` + `credit_ledger grant(topup, +12mo, stripe_ref)` + replay no-op + `topup.purchased`/`wtp.recorded{source:"purchase"}` rows. | Everything except Stripe origination proven live on prod 2026-09-18 (see `docs/build/runs/PRB.17.md` § Stripe leg): signature verify → unique-id insert → `stripe.event` consumer → idempotent grant; `rhema:stripePaymentLinkUrl` pinned to a clearly-marked placeholder (`test_placeholder_pending_real_link`) until a real link exists. **2026-09-18 (PRB.18):** the live-mode leg ran — real `sk_live_` key minted endpoint `we_1UH99C2MLe3ETP9Sk4UQBKXs` (`api.rhema.tools/stripe/webhook`, enabled, livemode, events `checkout.session.completed`+`charge.dispute.*`), its whsec seeded as `rhema-prod-stripe-webhook-secret` **v2** (21:16:48), live Payment Link `plink_1UH99P2MLe3ETP9SR1zL2jAW` ($25) written to `rhema:stripePaymentLinkUrl` + rolled (api `…-00022-cwx`; `/billing/balance` serves it). ## PRB.20 — live ingestion + post-launch live-use findings

| id | obligation | why deferred | closes when | verification | evidence | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| D-PRB.20-1 | **No concurrent-run guard — repeated run-sheet submissions spawn parallel runs + parallel holds.** The sheet mints `idempotencyKey` per GET render (`buildSheet` → `randomUUID()`), so the memo dedupe protects retries of ONE rendered submission but each sheet visit is a fresh intent; the api has no active-run check. Live result (operator, 2026-09-22): 4 review runs on `my-first-project` created 16:47:44–55Z, each holding 546 cr — 2,184 cr of a 2,500 balance held, 3 runs parked on unresolved `approve_scope` gates. | The concurrency question is a design ruling, not a mechanical fix: are parallel runs on one project ever legitimate (P1.06 scoped runs suggest per-scope parallelism may be)? The fix shape follows the ruling — an active-run guard in `createRun` (per project / per draft / per scope), the sheet showing "run in progress" instead of a fresh Run affordance, or both. | A ticket deciding the concurrency policy + implementing the guard (candidate: fold into P1.05/P1.06 run-card work, or a standalone PRB.2x defect ticket if the operator wants it sooner). | Submit the run sheet twice against the same project: second submit surfaces "run in progress" (or is rejected/redirected to the active run) per the ruling; assert no second hold lands in `credit_ledger`. | Live evidence: runs `01M35089QD…`, `01M3508D0Z…`, `01M3508EJS…`, `01M3508M00…` (2026-09-22, project `01M34YDZ3S…`) — 4× `review/quick`, 4× 546 cr holds; remediation = Stop on each of the 3 gate-parked dupes' `approve_scope` cards (cancels run + releases hold). | **DONE (2026-09-22, PRB.21)** — conservative beta ruling landed: at most ONE non-terminal run per project, enforced in `createRun` under a `select … for update` project-row lock (check-then-insert cannot race; memo dedupe precedes the guard so a same-key replay still returns its live run). Live on prod (update 69): two parallel `POST /projects/:id/runs` → 201 + **409 `run_in_progress`** carrying the active id; DB asserts ONE run row + ONE 36-cr `hold` (released on cancel); the sheet renders "in progress" + the `/review/<runId>` link with no Run affordance; `GET /projects` rows carry `activeRun`. Evidence: `docs/build/runs/PRB.21.md` § Stage B. |

## PRB.21 — post-launch live-use findings (day one cont.)

| id | obligation | why deferred | closes when | verification | evidence | status |
|----|------------|--------------|-------------|--------------|----------|--------|
| D-PRB.21-1 | **Estimator cap model cannot complete a real `review` run — verification-phase research burn is unmodeled.** The cap is enforced on proxy-metered burn (all SDK requests incl. research fetches' context), while the estimate tracks committed-debit rates. Live result (operator, 2026-09-22): FIVE consecutive `cap_reached` deaths across three rigor levels on two docs — `01M3508M` quick est 364/cap 546 → died 622; `01M35CEQ…` standard est 1657/cap 2486 → died 2629; `01M35FR9…` quick est 73/cap 110 → died 119; `01M35HHJ6…` quick est 73/cap 400 → died 407; `01M35KCFA…` quick est 73/cap 400 → died 466 on a 701-word draft with **zero fetchable URLs** (`fetch_log`: 128 fetches + 13 searches — verification does its own web research per claim regardless of in-doc citations; `usage_records`: 6.65M cache-read tokens / 41 calls). Meanwhile committed debits stayed modest (intake ~102, craft ~87–122). The one completed quick review (`01M2TZ8E`, cap 390, spent 146) predates the fetch-heavy verification profile — and quick's honest floor (est ~57–73 → cap ~86–110) sits BELOW minimum viable burn (~146+), so quick rigor is structurally uncompletable at derived caps. | The fix is a design ruling with real product/economic consequences: bound the research (per-claim/per-run fetch+search budget, fetched-page truncation/context discipline), model the burn (research addend in the estimator driven by claim count), and/or re-scope what the cap meters. Not a drive-by patch. | A ticket that picks the fix shape and lands it (candidate `28m_PRB.22`), then proves a real review DELIVERS within its derived cap on prod. | One bounded live `review` run on a small doc: assert `state=delivered`, `spent_credits ≤ cap_credits`, committed debits ≈ estimate, and fetch/search counts within the chosen budget. | Run ids above + ledger rows (holds 110/400/546/2486 → releases after debits 58/190/224/~158/536); `fetch_log`/`usage_records` counts from `rhema` prod DB 2026-09-22; ~$11.7 committed across the day's attempts. | **DONE — 2026-09-23 (delivered; residual pricing-model question stays OPEN for G3 below). History — 2026-09-22 PRB.22 sharpened it, then corrected it:** the research bound shipped live (per-run `webTools{fetchBudget:24,searchBudget:11,fetchMaxContentTokens:8000}` propagated to the proxy credential + estimator research addend → derived cap 207) but the one sanctioned verify run `01M35Q8M…` still died `cap_reached` at 211 — with **zero** fetches/searches made. Post-mortem usage analysis: ~34 requests × ~66K–1M cache-read tokens each ≈ 5.1M cache-read on a 1K-token doc — the burn is **baseline harness context churn** (protocol prompts + accumulated turns per request), independent of doc size or research. Correction to the earlier note: the "completed precedent" `01M2TZ8E` was a `fact_check` run (not review) and itself burned ~5.8M cache-read/~430cr usage — the pattern is inherent, and **no `review` run has ever delivered on prod within a derived cap**. Real fixed overhead ≈ 400–850cr burn per run; the estimator models content size, not request-count × context-size. Remaining work: context-management (per-phase fresh context / per-claim subagent isolation / prompt slimming) + a phase-count × per-phase-floor estimate model, OR an explicit product ruling on real per-run cost (~$4–8.50 burn) vs advertised estimate. **2026-09-23 addendum (PRB.23 Stage B):** sixth `cap_reached` death — `01M35Y9BH…` review/quick on the same zero-URL draft, deployed with the −45% request-envelope cut, frozen phase toggles, and the 50cr/phase churn term (derived est 288 / cap 432, no operator raise). Died in `verification` at proxy-metered 506 (committed debits only 159); usage: 5.03M cache-read over 44 calls (~60K/request to the editor loop), 13 fetches / 3 searches, 2 compactions. Confirms the residual is **per-phase transcript accumulation** — smaller per-request envelopes don't bound transcript growth; the churn term priced but under-modeled verification (≈347cr actual vs 50 priced). Next lever is context resets/per-claim isolation or a product cost ruling, not further envelope work. **2026-09-23 RESOLVED — `01M36663QG1ZJ7VEZ036Y54NSG` DELIVERED:** review/quick on draft 6 `completed` at 04:25Z, metered 720 ≤ operator cap 1100 (derived est 522/cap 783 — synthesis-phase `letter` burn still needed ~300cr of headroom above the derived cap; the derived cap alone would have died at 783 again). All phases done: intake 79, craft 70, verification 233 (bounded — first completion), argument 84, letter 120; committed debits 586 ($5.86); usage 104 reqs / 13.3M cache-read, 26f/27s, 1 compaction. **Residual sharpened, not closed:** the honest delivered cost of a 701-word quick review is ~720cr metered (~$7.20) vs sheet-advertised ~73cr-era pricing — the derived-cap vs delivered-cost gap (783 vs 720 marginal) and the committed-vs-metered delta (586 vs 720) remain the open pricing-model question for G3; synthesis-phase churn is still under-modeled (letter needed ~241 metered vs 120 committed). **2026-09-25 (RCN.1):** row stays DONE (the blocker — no real `review` could complete under the cap model — is resolved: `01M36663…` delivered). The residual pricing-model question is real but is *new* scope, not this row's AC; it rides **STB.3**'s bounded runs as metered-vs-committed data (and a future pricing-model ticket if STB.3's data confirms the gap). **2026-09-26 (STB.3 data):** the gap is confirmed alive — tonight committed 451cr vs 1,304cr metered (cancelled runs commit only completed-phase debits; `runs.spent_credits` carries the reconciled metered truth — §4-F5). New datapoint of a different class: the memo review `01M3DKQR0TY6ADGV2D1RTJBVG5` did NOT die on the cap model — it died on the `second_read_producer_checked` predicate defect (§4-F2, 438cr metered / 114 committed); whether a memo-protocol review can deliver within cap is still untested. Row stays DONE (AC met at RCN.1); the pricing-model + F2/F5 findings carry forward. |

against that evidence, so **GATE G1** (§17 Phase-0 exit) can be read out honestly. Three buckets:

(marker "DONE (2026-09-15) — reconciled…"); the rest are reconciled DONE here (their in-table row-flip is
clerical and rides their next touch):

Every counter write commits `held ≥ 0`; the ledger stays append-only. (`DEFERRABLE INITIALLY DEFERRED` — the ticket's alternate option — is impossible: Postgres CHECKs cannot be deferred; ordering is the fix.) Original status: OPEN — 2026-09-26 (DEF.2 Stage B finding). |
